1694    fn put_mutex(&self, collection_id: &[u8; 16]) -> Arc<parking_lot::Mutex<()>> {
1695        let mut locks = self.put_locks.lock();
1696        locks.entry(*collection_id).or_default().clone()
1697    }
1698
1699    fn read_at(
1700        &self,
1701        shard: &Arc<Shard>,
1702        offset: u64,
1703        verify: bool,
1704    ) -> Result<Record, StorageError> {
1705        self.shards.read_at(shard, offset, verify)
1706    }
1707
1708    /// The record's actual on-disk byte length (see
1709    /// [`ShardPool::record_disk_len_at`]) — the true disk-usage figure,
1710    /// unlike `Record::serialized_len()` which is an uncompressed upper
1711    /// bound.
1712    fn record_disk_len_at(shard: &Shard, offset: u64) -> Result<u64, StorageError> {
1713        ShardPool::record_disk_len_at(shard, offset)
1714    }
1715
1716    fn scan_collection_records(
1717        &self,
1718        collection_id: &[u8; 16],
1719    ) -> Result<Vec<ScannedShard>, StorageError> {
1720        let mut scanned: Vec<ScannedShard> = Vec::new();
1721        for (shard_id, shard) in self.shards.all_shards() {
1722            match packfile::scan_packfile(&shard.path) {
1723                Ok(entries) => {
1724                    let collection_entries: Vec<([u8; 16], u64)> = entries
1725                        .into_iter()
1726                        .filter(|(rid, _, _)| rid == collection_id)
1727                        .map(|(_, hash, offset)| (hash, offset))
1728                        .collect();
1729                    if !collection_entries.is_empty() {
1730                        scanned.push((shard_id, collection_entries));
1731                    }
1732                }
1733                Err(e) => {
1734                    return Err(StorageError::Io(e));
1735                }
1736            }
1737        }
1738        Ok(scanned)
1739    }
1740
1741    /// Scan every open shard once and build deduplicated record maps for the
1742    /// requested collections. This is the batch counterpart to `scan_collection_records`:
1743    /// a shard-compaction preflight must not reread the same packfile once per
1744    /// collection in its closure.
1745    fn scan_collection_record_maps(
1746        &self,
1747        collection_ids: &[[u8; 16]],
1748    ) -> Result<HashMap<[u8; 16], RepackRecordMap>, StorageError> {
1749        let wanted: HashSet<[u8; 16]> = collection_ids.iter().copied().collect();
1750        let mut maps: HashMap<[u8; 16], RepackRecordMap> = wanted
1751            .iter()
1752            .copied()
1753            .map(|collection_id| (collection_id, HashMap::new()))
1754            .collect();
1755        for (shard_id, shard) in self.shards.all_shards() {
1756            for (collection_id, hash, offset) in packfile::scan_packfile(&shard.path)? {
1757                if wanted.contains(&collection_id) {
1758                    maps.entry(collection_id)
1759                        .or_default()
1760                        .insert(hash, (shard_id, offset));
1761                }
1762            }
1763        }
1764        Ok(maps)
1765    }
1766
1767    fn build_index(offsets: &[([u8; 16], u16, u64)]) -> Result<LossyIndex, StorageError> {
1768        let index = LossyIndex::new(
1769            offsets
1770                .len()
1771                .saturating_mul(2)
1772                .max(NEW_COLLECTION_INDEX_FLOOR),
1773        );
1774        for (hash, shard_id, offset) in offsets {
1775            check_index_offset(*shard_id, hash, *offset)?;
1776            let _ = index.insert(hash, *shard_id, *offset);
1777        }
1778        Ok(index)
1779    }
1780
1781    /// Convert a slot-keyed `shard_counts` from `LossyIndex` to a pack_id-keyed
1782    /// `HashMap<u64, u64>` for use with `replace_collection_shard_counts`.
1783    fn slot_counts_to_pack_id_counts(&self, counts: &HashMap<u16, u64>) -> HashMap<u64, u64> {
1784        let shards = self.shards.all_shards();
1785        let slot_to_pack_id: HashMap<u16, u64> = shards
1786            .iter()
1787            .map(|(slot, shard)| (*slot, shard.pack_id))
1788            .collect();
1789        counts
1790            .iter()
1791            .map(|(&slot, &count)| {
1792                let pack_id = slot_to_pack_id
1793                    .get(&slot)
1794                    .copied()
1795                    .unwrap_or(u64::from(slot));
1796                (pack_id, count)
1797            })
1798            .collect()
1799    }
1800
1801    fn deleted_collections_path(base_dir: &std::path::Path) -> PathBuf {
1802        base_dir.join("deleted.collections")
1803    }
1804
1805    fn load_deleted_collections(base_dir: &std::path::Path) -> HashSet<[u8; 16]> {
1806        let path = Self::deleted_collections_path(base_dir);
1807        let Ok(bytes) = fs::read(&path) else {
1808            return HashSet::new();
1809        };
1810        bytes
1811            .chunks_exact(16)
1812            .map(|chunk| {
1813                let mut id = [0u8; 16];
1814                id.copy_from_slice(chunk);
1815                id
1816            })
1817            .collect()
1818    }
1819
1820    fn persist_deleted_collection(&self, collection_id: &[u8; 16]) -> Result<(), StorageError> {
1821        let path = Self::deleted_collections_path(&self.base_dir);
1822        let mut set = self.deleted_collections.lock();
1823        set.insert(*collection_id);
1824        let bytes: Vec<u8> = set.iter().flat_map(|id| id.iter().copied()).collect();
1825        fs::write(&path, &bytes).map_err(StorageError::Io)?;
1826        Ok(())
1827    }
1828
1829    /// Fast path for `store_generation`'s new-collection case: most
1830    /// collections were never deleted, so check the in-memory cache before
1831    /// paying for a lock + no-op write. Avoids re-reading `deleted.collections`
1832    /// from disk on every first write to a new collection — previously this
1833    /// reloaded the whole file from disk unconditionally, which under a cold
1834    /// page cache turned a batch of first-writes across many collections into
1835    /// one serialized disk read per collection.
1836    fn is_deleted_collection(&self, collection_id: &[u8; 16]) -> bool {
1837        self.deleted_collections.lock().contains(collection_id)
1838    }
1839
1840    fn clear_deleted_collection(&self, collection_id: &[u8; 16]) -> Result<(), StorageError> {
1841        let path = Self::deleted_collections_path(&self.base_dir);
1842        let mut set = self.deleted_collections.lock();
1843        if set.remove(collection_id) {
1844            let bytes: Vec<u8> = set.iter().flat_map(|id| id.iter().copied()).collect();
1845            fs::write(&path, &bytes).map_err(StorageError::Io)?;
1846        }
1847        Ok(())
1848    }
1849
1850    /// Records one new record landing in `shard_id` for `collection_id` — the
1851    /// cheap, O(1) path for a plain `put` that only appends, never moves
1852    /// or drops anything. Kept separate from
1853    /// [`Self::replace_collection_shard_counts`], which pays for a full
1854    /// per-shard recount and is reserved for the cases that actually
1855    /// change a collection's existing distribution (an index rebuild or a
1856    /// repack), so a normal write never regresses to O(collection size).
1857    fn record_new_shard_collection(&self, pack_id: u64, collection_id: &[u8; 16]) {
1858        let mut shard_collections = self.shard_collections.write();
1859        let count = shard_collections
1860            .entry(pack_id)
1861            .or_default()
1862            .entry(*collection_id)
1863            .or_insert(0);
1864        *count = count.saturating_add(1);
1865        drop(shard_collections);
1866        self.collection_shards
1867            .write()
1868            .entry(*collection_id)
1869            .or_default()
1870            .insert(pack_id);
1871    }
1872
1873    /// Replaces `collection_id`'s entire contribution to `shard_collections` with
1874    /// `counts` (typically `LossyIndex::shard_counts()` converted to `pack_id` keys)
1875    /// — clears it out of any shard it no longer occupies and installs the fresh
1876    /// per-shard counts. Used wherever a collection's index is replaced wholesale
1877    /// rather than incrementally appended to, since only then can its distribution
1878    /// across shards actually change.
1879    fn replace_collection_shard_counts(
1880        &self,
1881        collection_id: &[u8; 16],
1882        counts: &HashMap<u64, u64>,
1883    ) {
1884        let old_shards = self
1885            .collection_shards
1886            .write()
1887            .insert(*collection_id, counts.keys().copied().collect());
1888        let mut shard_collections = self.shard_collections.write();
1889        if let Some(old_shards) = old_shards {
1890            for pack_id in &old_shards {
1891                if !counts.contains_key(pack_id) {
1892                    if let Some(m) = shard_collections.get_mut(pack_id) {
1893                        m.remove(collection_id);
1894                        if m.is_empty() {
1895                            shard_collections.remove(pack_id);
1896                        }
1897                    }
1898                }
1899            }
1900        }
1901        for (&pack_id, &count) in counts {
1902            shard_collections
1903                .entry(pack_id)
1904                .or_default()
1905                .insert(*collection_id, count);
1906        }
1907    }
1908
1909    /// Removes `collection_id` from `shard_collections`/`collection_shards` entirely —
1910    /// used on collection deletion, where nothing of the collection survives in any
1911    /// shard.
1912    fn remove_collection_shard_counts(&self, collection_id: &[u8; 16]) {
1913        let Some(old_shards) = self.collection_shards.write().remove(collection_id) else {
1914            return;
1915        };
1916        let mut shard_collections = self.shard_collections.write();
1917        for pack_id in old_shards {
1918            if let Some(m) = shard_collections.get_mut(&pack_id) {
1919                m.remove(collection_id);
1920                if m.is_empty() {
1921                    shard_collections.remove(&pack_id);
1922                }
1923            }
1924        }
1925    }
1926
1927    /// Path to the persisted shard→collection directory for a base directory.
1928    fn shard_collections_path(base_dir: &std::path::Path) -> PathBuf {
1929        base_dir.join("shard_collections.bin")
1930    }
1931
1932    /// Persist the current `shard_collections` directory to disk: which collections
1933    /// have live records in each shard, and how many. Read by
1934    /// [`Self::collection_directory_from_disk`] — a free function a separate,
1935    /// short-lived process (a `collections`-style CLI listing) can call without
1936    /// opening a full `PackfileStorage` (which would otherwise mean
1937    /// scanning and index-building every shard just to answer "what collections
1938    /// exist and how big are they").
1939    ///
1940    /// Writes to a temp file and renames into place, same crash-safety
1941    /// pattern as `ShardPool::persist_stats`.
1942    ///
1943    /// The header pins the directory to the `pack_fingerprint` of the
1944    /// current committed pack set. Only the caller writing right after
1945    /// flush+fsync (the index-checkpoint rewrite) is guaranteed to produce a
1946    /// fingerprint matching the checkpoint's, which is what lets open rebuild
1947    /// per-shard counts from these records instead of walking every slot; any
1948    /// other write leaves a directory that open either accepts (pack set
1949    /// unchanged) or safely falls back from.
1950    ///
1951    /// # Errors
1952    /// Returns `StorageError` on write or rename failure.
1953    pub fn persist_shard_collections(&self) -> Result<(), StorageError> {
1954        let mut buf = Vec::new();
1955        buf.extend_from_slice(SHARD_ROOMS_MAGIC);
1956        buf.push(SHARD_ROOMS_VERSION);
1957        let packs: Vec<(u64, u64)> = self
1958            .shards
1959            .all_shards()
1960            .into_iter()
1961            .map(|(_, shard)| (shard.pack_id, shard.file_len()))
1962            .collect();
1963        let fingerprint = crate::index::checkpoint::pack_fingerprint(&packs);
1964        buf.extend_from_slice(&fingerprint.to_le_bytes());
1965        let persisted_at = std::time::SystemTime::now()
1966            .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
1967            .map_or(0, |d| d.as_secs());
1968        buf.extend_from_slice(&persisted_at.to_le_bytes());
1969        let collection_order: HashMap<[u8; 16], u64> = self
1970            .collection_order
1971            .read()
1972            .iter()
1973            .enumerate()
1974            .map(|(index, collection_id)| {
1975                u64::try_from(index)
1976                    .map(|index| (*collection_id, index))
1977                    .map_err(|_| {
1978                        StorageError::Io(std::io::Error::other("collection order exceeds u64"))
1979                    })
1980            })
1981            .collect::<Result<_, _>>()?;
1982        for (pack_id, collections) in self.shard_collections.read().iter() {
1983            for (collection_id, count) in collections {
1984                buf.extend_from_slice(&pack_id.to_le_bytes());
1985                buf.extend_from_slice(collection_id);
1986                buf.extend_from_slice(&count.to_le_bytes());
1987                let order = collection_order
1988                    .get(collection_id)
1989                    .copied()
1990                    .unwrap_or(u64::MAX);
1991                buf.extend_from_slice(&order.to_le_bytes());
1992            }
1993        }
1994
1995        let unique = SHARD_ROOMS_TMP_COUNTER.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
1996        let tmp_path = Self::shard_collections_path(&self.base_dir)
1997            .with_extension(format!("bin.tmp.{}.{unique}", std::process::id()));
1998        let final_path = Self::shard_collections_path(&self.base_dir);
1999        let write_result = (|| -> std::io::Result<()> {
2000            let mut tmp = fs::File::create(&tmp_path)?;
2001            std::io::Write::write_all(&mut tmp, &buf)?;
2002            // See the matching comment in `checkpoint::write_checkpoint`:
2003            // this tmp file is renamed away immediately below and never
2004            // reopened by this name, so only its data need survive —
2005            // `sync_data` (fdatasync) skips the timestamp-only metadata
2006            // flush `sync_all` (fsync) would also perform.
2007            tmp.sync_data()
2008        })();
2009        if let Err(e) = write_result {
2010            let _ = fs::remove_file(&tmp_path);
2011            return Err(StorageError::Io(e));
2012        }
2013        fs::rename(&tmp_path, &final_path).map_err(StorageError::Io)?;
2014        Ok(())
2015    }
2016
2017    /// Best-effort wrapper around [`Self::persist_shard_collections`] — logs and
2018    /// swallows a failure rather than turning it into a hard error, same
2019    /// contract as `ShardPool`'s stats persistence: this is observability
2020    /// data, not something worth failing an otherwise-successful sync
2021    /// over.
2022    fn persist_shard_collections_best_effort(&self) {
2023        if let Err(e) = self.persist_shard_collections() {
2024            eprintln!("mtxdb: failed to persist shard→collection directory: {e}");
2025        }
2026    }
2027
2028    /// Mark that the pending delta log can no longer be continued: a
2029    /// structural change (capacity growth, full rebuild, repack, refresh, or a
2030    /// collection create/delete) means replay onto the last checkpoint can't
2031    /// reconstruct the new state, so the next sync must do a full checkpoint
2032    /// rewrite (which re-bases the log) instead of an append.
2033    fn invalidate_delta_log(&self) {
2034        self.delta_invalidations.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
2035        self.delta_state.lock().invalid = true;
2036    }
2037
2038    /// Record a successful index mutation as a delta frame, if the delta log
2039    /// can legitimately continue through it.
2040    ///
2041    /// Recording is suppressed whenever continuing the log is unsafe: after a
2042    /// structural invalidation, or when the frame's collection isn't part of
2043    /// the log's base checkpoint or has since changed shape (its live
2044    /// generation no longer equals the base's). A suppressed frame simply
2045    /// leaves the log unable to cover the write; the next sync notices the
2046    /// gap (missing frames for some collection ⇒ base generation mismatch
2047    /// recorded at checkpoint-decode time forces a full rewrite) and re-bases.
2048    fn record_delta(&self, collection_id: &[u8; 16], generation: u64, bucket: u32, slot: u64) {
2049        let mut state = self.delta_state.lock();
2050        if state.invalid {
2051            return;
2052        }
2053        if state.base_generations.get(collection_id) != Some(&generation) {
2054            return;
2055        }
2056        state.pending.push(DeltaFrame {
2057            collection_id: *collection_id,
2058            bucket,
2059            generation,
2060            slot,
2061        });
2062    }
2063
2064    /// Persist the full per-collection index state to `index.checkpoint`, so
2065    /// the next open can load it instead of rescanning every packfile.
2066    ///
2067    /// Called after every sync barrier — packfile data first, checkpoint
2068    /// second. A crash in between leaves a fingerprint mismatch that the next
2069    /// open resolves with a rescan; a crash after leaves a valid checkpoint.
2070    ///
2071    /// The rewrite hands off the delta log across two epochs: the session
2072    /// keeps appending to the log that continues the *old* checkpoint (D0)
2073    /// right up to the rotation below, after which every `put` is recorded
2074    /// against a fresh epoch (D1) named for the new checkpoint's fingerprint.
2075    /// D0's file is untouched through the rotation and retired only once the
2076    /// new checkpoint (C1) is durably renamed, so a crash at any point pairs
2077    /// the loader with whichever log legitimately continues the checkpoint on
2078    /// disk: before C1's rename C0 + D0 is authoritative, after it C1 + D1.
2079    /// The log's fingerprint-based name is itself the safety gate — a stale
2080    /// predecessor (D0) or an orphaned successor (D1) can never match the
2081    /// checkpoint a reopen loads (see [`Self::delta_path`]).
2082    ///
2083    /// Cheap no-op when no collection data has changed since the last write
2084    /// (`index_checkpoint_dirty` cleared on success), so a writer that syncs
2085    /// repeatedly without writes doesn't rewrite the acceleration file. The
2086    /// flag is cleared only when nothing after this rewrite's snapshot is
2087    /// left unpersisted (see the lock re-acquisition below), so a transient
2088    /// failure or a racing write defers rather than drops the update.
2089    fn persist_index_checkpoint(&self) -> Result<(), StorageError> {
2090        if !self.index_checkpoint_dirty.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
2091            return Ok(());
2092        }
2093        // Epoch-handoff protocol: hold every collection's put_mutex only
2094        // across the snapshot + epoch rotation below (same sorted-lock
2095        // pattern as `retire_empty_shards_after_batch`), then release it
2096        // before the comparatively slow serialize + fsync + rename. This is
2097        // safe — where releasing right after the fingerprint snapshot alone
2098        // was *not* (see git history on this function) — because rotation
2099        // switches every future `put`'s delta frames onto a fresh epoch
2100        // (named for the fingerprint captured here) before the locks drop,
2101        // rather than leaving them targeting an epoch that's about to be
2102        // silently discarded:
2103        //
2104        //   - Every put after this point is recorded, if at all, against the
2105        //     *new* epoch (D1), never the old one (D0) — `rotate_delta_epoch`
2106        //     runs inside the same locked section as the fingerprint/index
2107        //     snapshot, so there is no window where a put's frame could still
2108        //     land on D0 after D0's corresponding checkpoint state has
2109        //     already been captured for D1's own checkpoint.
2110        //   - D0's on-disk file is left completely untouched by the rotation
2111        //     — only retired (deleted) after the new checkpoint (C1) is
2112        //     durably renamed, below. So a crash before C1 exists leaves the
2113        //     old checkpoint (C0) still correctly paired with D0.
2114        //   - D1 is only ever trusted by a reopen if it names the exact
2115        //     fingerprint of the checkpoint that reopen loads (see
2116        //     `delta_path`) — so a crash after rotation but before C1 is
2117        //     durable leaves D1 orphaned (no checkpoint names its
2118        //     fingerprint) and correctly ignored; the reopen uses C0 + D0.
2119        //
2120        // `RoomGeneration` is immutable once published (COW, swapped
2121        // atomically), so an owned `Arc` clone taken under the lock is as
2122        // good a snapshot as the live one for serialization purposes.
2123        let mut collection_ids: Vec<[u8; 16]> = self.collections.read().keys().copied().collect();
2124        collection_ids.sort_unstable();
2125        // The guards borrow these `Arc`s, so the handles must outlive the
2126        // guards; this vector is also re-used for the end-of-function
2127        // dirty-check re-lock so late-added collections stay pinned too.
2128        let mut lock_arcs: Vec<_> = collection_ids.iter().map(|id| self.put_mutex(id)).collect();
2129
2130        // Exclude new-collection publication from the fingerprint→snapshot
2131        // window. A put *to* an existing collection is already gated by that
2132        // collection's `put_mutex`, but a put that is about to *create* a
2133        // collection holds no mutex this function acquired, so its record
2134        // frame could be Eager-flushed into a pack between our `flush_all` and
2135        // our fingerprint read — the fingerprint would then name a record
2136        // whose collection never made it into the snapshot, and a reopen that
2137        // trusts C1 would silently drop it. Publishing puts hold this lock
2138        // shared across the entire put; acquiring it exclusive here (before
2139        // the fingerprint, held through the rotation) means no such put can
2140        // interleave its flush with our capture: it either finished
2141        // beforehand (its collection is in the snapshot) or runs entirely
2142        // afterwards (its bytes land post-fingerprint, and the fingerprint
2143        // mismatch on reopen routes to the full rescan).
2144        let create_guard = self.collection_creation.write();
2145        // A collection created between the initial scan above and this lock
2146        // wasn't in the locked set and must be now — otherwise a put to one
2147        // serially dispatched by the engine could still squeeze under the
2148        // fingerprint while its publication was snapshotted.
2149        let ids_now: Vec<[u8; 16]> = self.collections.read().keys().copied().collect();
2150        for id in ids_now.iter().filter(|id| !collection_ids.contains(id)) {
2151            lock_arcs.push(self.put_mutex(id));
2152        }
2153        // Now build the lock guards: the arcs must outlive them (collected
2154        // in `lock_arcs` above and reused below for the dirty-check).
2155        let guards: Vec<_> = lock_arcs.iter().map(|arc| arc.lock()).collect();
2156
2157        // Commit any buffered frames first: the fingerprint below pins each
2158        // shard to its committed on-disk length, and the serialized index
2159        // offsets must be readable against exactly that length on the next
2160        // open. Writing the checkpoint while records are still buffered
2161        // would persist virtual offsets the fingerprint can't describe.
2162        self.shards.flush_all()?;
2163        let packs: Vec<(u64, u64)> = self
2164            .shards
2165            .all_shards()
2166            .into_iter()
2167            .map(|(_, shard)| (shard.pack_id, shard.file_len()))
2168            .collect();
2169        let fingerprint = crate::index::checkpoint::pack_fingerprint(&packs);
2170
2171        let order = self.collection_order.read().clone();
2172        let snapshots: Vec<([u8; 16], u64, Arc<RoomGeneration>)> = {
2173            let collections = self.collections.read();
2174            order
2175                .iter()
2176                .filter_map(|collection_id| {
2177                    collections.get(collection_id).map(|g| {
2178                        let generation = arc_swap::ArcSwapAny::load_full(g);
2179                        (*collection_id, generation.generation, generation)
2180                    })
2181                })
2182                .collect()
2183        };
2184
2185        // Rotate the delta epoch while still locked: `old_base_fingerprint`
2186        // (D0's name, if any) is retired below only after `fingerprint`'s
2187        // checkpoint (C1) is durable; every collection's writers already
2188        // target the new epoch (D1) by the time the locks drop next.
2189        let old_base_fingerprint = self.rotate_delta_epoch(fingerprint, &snapshots);
2190        drop(guards);
2191        // `create_guard` is deliberately NOT dropped here, unlike the
2192        // per-collection put mutexes above. An existing collection's
2193        // concurrent put after this point is safe to let through: its frame
2194        // lands after `fingerprint` and is recovered via the new delta epoch
2195        // (D1) on reopen — see `rotate_delta_epoch`. A *newly discovered*
2196        // collection (via `put`/`put_many` creating one, or `refresh_collection`
2197        // publishing one from external writes) has no such fallback: it
2198        // doesn't go through delta tracking, so if it publishes into
2199        // `self.collections` during the unlocked serialize+write+rename
2200        // below, its data can already be covered by `fingerprint` (the
2201        // packs it lives in were flushed above) while being absent from
2202        // `entries`/`blobs` (snapshotted just above, before it existed) —
2203        // a crash after the rename makes reopen trust a checkpoint that
2204        // silently omits it. Keep new-collection publication excluded until
2205        // the checkpoint is durably renamed.
2206
2207        let entries: Vec<([u8; 16], u64, Vec<u8>)> = snapshots
2208            .iter()
2209            .map(|(collection_id, generation, room)| {
2210                (*collection_id, *generation, room.index.serialize())
2211            })
2212            .collect();
2213        let blobs: Vec<([u8; 16], u64, &[u8])> = entries
2214            .iter()
2215            .map(|(collection_id, generation, blob)| (*collection_id, *generation, blob.as_slice()))
2216            .collect();
2217        crate::index::checkpoint::write_checkpoint(
2218            &Self::index_checkpoint_path(&self.base_dir),
2219            fingerprint,
2220            &blobs,
2221        )
2222        .map_err(StorageError::Io)?;
2223        // `write_checkpoint` fsyncs the new checkpoint's own bytes before
2224        // renaming it into place, but the rename itself — the directory
2225        // entry now pointing at the new inode — is only durable once the
2226        // directory's own metadata is synced. Do that explicitly here rather
2227        // than relying on `retire_delta_epoch`'s fsync (below) to cover it
2228        // incidentally: that one only runs, and only after this rename, when
2229        // there was a prior epoch to retire (`old_base_fingerprint.is_some()`
2230        // and its `remove_file` succeeds) — a first-ever checkpoint, or a
2231        // failed unlink, would otherwise leave the rename's durability
2232        // resting on a later, unrelated sync happening to occur. Best-effort:
2233        // a failure here is still safe, since a reopen after a crash before
2234        // this fsync commits either observes the rename (fingerprint gate
2235        // passes) or doesn't (falls back to the still-valid predecessor
2236        // checkpoint) — never a torn or partially-visible rename.
2237        let _ = fs::File::open(&self.base_dir).and_then(|dir| dir.sync_all());
2238        // The checkpoint naming `fingerprint` is now durably in place, so a
2239        // new collection publishing from here on lands after it — same
2240        // recovery story a reopen already has for any other post-checkpoint
2241        // write (full rescan on fingerprint mismatch, or a fresh delta
2242        // epoch). Safe to let new-collection publication through now.
2243        drop(create_guard);
2244        self.retire_delta_epoch(old_base_fingerprint);
2245        // The unlocked serialize/write window above let concurrent puts land
2246        // after the rotation. C1 was snapshotted before them, so such a put
2247        // is durable only as a pending delta frame — if any frames, or a new
2248        // invalidation, are still unapplied the dirty flag must survive so
2249        // the next sync appends them to D1 instead of stranding them in
2250        // memory. Briefly re-acquire every put_mutex (no I/O under them) so
2251        // this decision can't interleave with a put's own frame-push +
2252        // dirty-set (see `put_many`; the frame goes in before the flag).
2253        // Re-lock the *extended* set (`lock_arcs` includes anything published
2254        // during the initial scan), so a put to a collection that entered the
2255        // locked window late is still pinned for this check.
2256        let guards = lock_arcs.iter().map(|arc| arc.lock()).collect::<Vec<_>>();
2257        let has_unfinished_work = {
2258            let state = self.delta_state.lock();
2259            !state.pending.is_empty() || state.invalid
2260        };
2261        if !has_unfinished_work {
2262            self.index_checkpoint_dirty.store(false, Ordering::Relaxed);
2263        }
2264        drop(guards);
2265        // Keep the inspection directory gated to the same pack set the
2266        // checkpoint just became: ride the checkpoint rewrite (best-effort)
2267        // so the next open can rebuild per-shard counts from records instead
2268        // of walking every slot. Deliberately not wired to bare sync_all —
2269        // after a dirty sync() the checkpoint advances to a new fingerprint
2270        // while a sync_all-only sidecar would stay stale, silently regressing
2271        // the very reopen this sidecar exists to speed up. A failure here
2272        // leaves the previous directory stale; the fingerprint gate then
2273        // falls back to the slot walk until the next rewrite.
2274        self.persist_shard_collections_best_effort();
2275        Ok(())
2276    }
2277
2278    /// Switch the live delta-log state onto a fresh epoch named for
2279    /// `fingerprint`, the checkpoint about to be written. Must be called
2280    /// while every collection's `put_mutex` is held (see
2281    /// `persist_index_checkpoint`), so every `put` from here on is recorded,
2282    /// if at all, against the new epoch — never the one this call is
2283    /// replacing. Returns the *previous* base fingerprint (the epoch to
2284    /// retire once the new checkpoint is durable), or `None` if this session
2285    /// had no prior epoch (opened via a full rescan).
2286    fn rotate_delta_epoch(
2287        &self,
2288        fingerprint: u64,
2289        snapshots: &[([u8; 16], u64, Arc<RoomGeneration>)],
2290    ) -> Option<u64> {
2291        let mut state = self.delta_state.lock();
2292        let old_base_fingerprint = state.base_fingerprint;
2293        state.base_fingerprint = Some(fingerprint);
2294        state.base_generations = snapshots
2295            .iter()
2296            .map(|(collection_id, generation, _)| (*collection_id, *generation))
2297            .collect();
2298        state.pending.clear();
2299        state.log_bytes = 0;
2300        state.invalid = false;
2301        old_base_fingerprint
2302    }
2303
2304    /// Test-only: a byte-for-byte mirror of `persist_index_checkpoint`,
2305    /// except it sleeps for `delay` right after releasing the locks (in
2306    /// place of, not in addition to, the production function's body — this
2307    /// is never called from non-test code and the production function is
2308    /// untouched). Lets a test make the unlocked window arbitrarily long
2309    /// without depending on a dataset large enough to make a real
2310    /// serialize+fsync slow relative to test-machine noise. See
2311    /// `test_put_does_not_block_on_slow_checkpoint_rewrite`.
2312    #[cfg(test)]
2313    fn test_persist_index_checkpoint_with_delay(
2314        &self,
2315        delay: std::time::Duration,
2316        entered_unlocked_window: &AtomicBool,
2317    ) -> Result<(), StorageError> {
2318        if !self.index_checkpoint_dirty.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
2319            entered_unlocked_window.store(true, Ordering::Release);
2320            return Ok(());
2321        }
2322        let mut collection_ids: Vec<[u8; 16]> = self.collections.read().keys().copied().collect();
2323        collection_ids.sort_unstable();
2324        let mut lock_arcs: Vec<_> = collection_ids.iter().map(|id| self.put_mutex(id)).collect();
2325        let create_guard = self.collection_creation.write();
2326        let ids_now: Vec<[u8; 16]> = self.collections.read().keys().copied().collect();
2327        for id in ids_now.iter().filter(|id| !collection_ids.contains(id)) {
2328            lock_arcs.push(self.put_mutex(id));
2329        }
2330        let guards: Vec<_> = lock_arcs.iter().map(|arc| arc.lock()).collect();
2331        self.shards.flush_all()?;
2332        let fingerprint = self.current_pack_fingerprint();
2333        let order = self.collection_order.read().clone();
2334        let snapshots: Vec<([u8; 16], u64, Arc<RoomGeneration>)> = {
2335            let collections = self.collections.read();
2336            order
2337                .iter()
2338                .filter_map(|collection_id| {
2339                    collections.get(collection_id).map(|g| {
2340                        let generation = arc_swap::ArcSwapAny::load_full(g);
2341                        (*collection_id, generation.generation, generation)
2342                    })
2343                })
2344                .collect()
2345        };
2346        let old_base_fingerprint = self.rotate_delta_epoch(fingerprint, &snapshots);
2347        drop(guards);
2348        drop(create_guard);
2349
2350        // The test waits on this: it now *knows* the rewrite is sleeping in
2351        // its unlocked window rather than guessing via a fixed sleep, so a
2352        // put issued from here provably overlaps the delay.
2353        entered_unlocked_window.store(true, Ordering::Release);
2354
2355        std::thread::sleep(delay);
2356
2357        let entries: Vec<([u8; 16], u64, Vec<u8>)> = snapshots
2358            .iter()
2359            .map(|(collection_id, generation, room)| {
2360                (*collection_id, *generation, room.index.serialize())
2361            })
2362            .collect();
2363        let blobs: Vec<([u8; 16], u64, &[u8])> = entries
2364            .iter()
2365            .map(|(collection_id, generation, blob)| (*collection_id, *generation, blob.as_slice()))
2366            .collect();
2367        crate::index::checkpoint::write_checkpoint(
2368            &Self::index_checkpoint_path(&self.base_dir),
2369            fingerprint,
2370            &blobs,
2371        )
2372        .map_err(StorageError::Io)?;
2373        let _ = fs::File::open(&self.base_dir).and_then(|dir| dir.sync_all());
2374        self.retire_delta_epoch(old_base_fingerprint);
2375        let guards = lock_arcs.iter().map(|m| m.lock()).collect::<Vec<_>>();
2376        let has_unfinished_work = {
2377            let state = self.delta_state.lock();
2378            !state.pending.is_empty() || state.invalid
2379        };
2380        if !has_unfinished_work {
2381            self.index_checkpoint_dirty.store(false, Ordering::Relaxed);
2382        }
2383        drop(guards);
2384        self.persist_shard_collections_best_effort();
2385        Ok(())
2386    }
2387
2388    /// Test-only: perform exactly the locked prefix of
2389    /// `persist_index_checkpoint` (flush, fingerprint, index snapshot, epoch
2390    /// rotation) and then stop, *never* writing or renaming a checkpoint.
2391    /// Simulates a crash between the D0→D1 rotation and C1's rename — the
2392    /// window where D1 exists only in memory and no on-disk checkpoint names
2393    /// its fingerprint yet.
2394    #[cfg(test)]
2395    fn test_rotate_epoch_without_checkpoint(&self) -> (u64, Option<u64>) {
2396        let mut collection_ids: Vec<[u8; 16]> = self.collections.read().keys().copied().collect();
2397        collection_ids.sort_unstable();
2398        let mutexes: Vec<_> = collection_ids.iter().map(|id| self.put_mutex(id)).collect();
2399        let _guards: Vec<_> = mutexes.iter().map(|m| m.lock()).collect();
2400        self.shards.flush_all().unwrap();
2401        let fingerprint = self.current_pack_fingerprint();
2402        let order = self.collection_order.read().clone();
2403        let snapshots: Vec<([u8; 16], u64, Arc<RoomGeneration>)> = {
2404            let collections = self.collections.read();
2405            order
2406                .iter()
2407                .filter_map(|collection_id| {
2408                    collections.get(collection_id).map(|g| {
2409                        let generation = arc_swap::ArcSwapAny::load_full(g);
2410                        (*collection_id, generation.generation, generation)
2411                    })
2412                })
2413                .collect()
2414        };
2415        let old_base_fingerprint = self.rotate_delta_epoch(fingerprint, &snapshots);
2416        (fingerprint, old_base_fingerprint)
2417    }

2419    /// Test-only: perform everything `persist_index_checkpoint` does up
2420    /// through the checkpoint rename and its directory fsync, but stop
2421    /// *before* `retire_delta_epoch`. Simulates a crash between C1 becoming
2422    /// durable and D0's retirement — the window where both C1+D1 (current)
2423    /// and C0's now-stale D0 (orphaned) exist on disk simultaneously.
2424    #[cfg(test)]
2425    fn test_write_checkpoint_without_retire(&self) -> Result<(u64, Option<u64>), StorageError> {
2426        let mut collection_ids: Vec<[u8; 16]> = self.collections.read().keys().copied().collect();
2427        collection_ids.sort_unstable();
2428        let mutexes: Vec<_> = collection_ids.iter().map(|id| self.put_mutex(id)).collect();
2429        let guards: Vec<_> = mutexes.iter().map(|m| m.lock()).collect();
2430        self.shards.flush_all()?;
2431        let fingerprint = self.current_pack_fingerprint();
2432        let order = self.collection_order.read().clone();
2433        let snapshots: Vec<([u8; 16], u64, Arc<RoomGeneration>)> = {
2434            let collections = self.collections.read();
2435            order
2436                .iter()
2437                .filter_map(|collection_id| {
2438                    collections.get(collection_id).map(|g| {
2439                        let generation = arc_swap::ArcSwapAny::load_full(g);
2440                        (*collection_id, generation.generation, generation)
2441                    })
2442                })
2443                .collect()
2444        };
2445        let old_base_fingerprint = self.rotate_delta_epoch(fingerprint, &snapshots);
2446        drop(guards);
2447        let entries: Vec<([u8; 16], u64, Vec<u8>)> = snapshots
2448            .iter()
2449            .map(|(collection_id, generation, room)| {
2450                (*collection_id, *generation, room.index.serialize())
2451            })
2452            .collect();
2453        let blobs: Vec<([u8; 16], u64, &[u8])> = entries
2454            .iter()
2455            .map(|(collection_id, generation, blob)| (*collection_id, *generation, blob.as_slice()))
2456            .collect();
2457        crate::index::checkpoint::write_checkpoint(
2458            &Self::index_checkpoint_path(&self.base_dir),
2459            fingerprint,
2460            &blobs,
2461        )
2462        .map_err(StorageError::Io)?;
2463        let _ = fs::File::open(&self.base_dir).and_then(|dir| dir.sync_all());
2464        Ok((fingerprint, old_base_fingerprint))
2465    }
2466
2467    /// Remove the now-superseded delta-log epoch, once the checkpoint that
2468    /// makes it safe to discard is durably renamed. A no-op if this session
2469    /// had no prior epoch. Best-effort: a failure here leaves the retired
2470    /// epoch's file on disk, which is always safe (see `delta_path`) — it
2471    /// simply names a fingerprint no future checkpoint will ever carry again.
2472    fn retire_delta_epoch(&self, old_base_fingerprint: Option<u64>) {
2473        let Some(old_base_fingerprint) = old_base_fingerprint else {
2474            return;
2475        };
2476        let path = Self::delta_path(&self.base_dir, old_base_fingerprint);
2477        if fs::remove_file(&path).is_ok() {
2478            // The checkpoint rename's own durability is already covered by
2479            // the caller's fsync right after `write_checkpoint` returns; this
2480            // one covers the unlink instead, so the retired epoch's file
2481            // doesn't linger past a crash — harmless either way (see the
2482            // doc comment above), but tidier.
2483            let _ = fs::File::open(&self.base_dir).and_then(|dir| dir.sync_all());
2484        }
2485    }
2486
2487    /// Append the pending delta frames as one framed, fsynced batch and clear
2488    /// the dirty flag. `tail_fingerprint` is the fingerprint of the pack set
2489    /// the frames were recorded against — computed by the caller after the
2490    /// flush that made those bytes durable. The on-disk log, if any, is
2491    /// continued; otherwise a fresh header pins `base_fingerprint` (the
2492    /// checkpoint the frames extend) for the reopen replay gate.
2493    fn append_index_delta(&self, tail_fingerprint: u64) -> Result<(), StorageError> {
2494        let mut state = self.delta_state.lock();
2495        let Some(base_fingerprint) = state.base_fingerprint else {
2496            return Err(StorageError::Io(std::io::Error::other(
2497                "delta append with no base fingerprint",
2498            )));
2499        };
2500        if state.invalid {
2501            return Err(StorageError::Io(std::io::Error::other(
2502                "delta append while the log is invalidated",
2503            )));
2504        }
2505        if state.pending.is_empty() {
2506            return Err(StorageError::Io(std::io::Error::other(
2507                "delta append with no pending frames",
2508            )));
2509        }
2510        let path = Self::delta_path(&self.base_dir, base_fingerprint);
2511        // A decoder deliberately stops at a torn or corrupt suffix and gives
2512        // us the sealed frontier. Before extending the log, discard that
2513        // suffix: appending after it would leave every new, valid batch behind
2514        // bytes a future decoder correctly refuses to cross.
2515        let on_disk_len = fs::metadata(&path)
2516            .map_or_else(
2517                |error| {
2518                    if error.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound {
2519                        Ok(0)
2520                    } else {
2521                        Err(error)
2522                    }
2523                },
2524                |metadata| Ok(metadata.len()),
2525            )
2526            .map_err(StorageError::Io)?;
2527        if on_disk_len < state.log_bytes {
2528            return Err(StorageError::Io(std::io::Error::other(
2529                "delta log shrank below its sealed frontier",
2530            )));
2531        }
2532        if on_disk_len > state.log_bytes {
2533            fs::OpenOptions::new()
2534                .write(true)
2535                .open(&path)
2536                .and_then(|file| file.set_len(state.log_bytes))
2537                .map_err(StorageError::Io)?;
2538        }
2539        let write_header = state.log_bytes == 0;
2540        // Do not charge a continuation for another header: the exact next
2541        // physical write is either one batch, or a header plus one batch.
2542        let batch_bytes = delta::batch_len(state.pending.len()).ok_or_else(|| {
2543            StorageError::Io(std::io::Error::other("delta batch length overflow"))
2544        })?;
2545        let next_len = state
2546            .log_bytes
2547            .saturating_add(u64::try_from(batch_bytes).unwrap_or(u64::MAX))
2548            .saturating_add(if write_header {
2549                DELTA_LOG_HEADER_LEN as u64
2550            } else {
2551                0
2552            });
2553        if next_len > DELTA_LOG_CAP_BYTES {
2554            return Err(StorageError::Io(std::io::Error::other(
2555                "delta log cap reached",
2556            )));
2557        }
2558        let pending = std::mem::take(&mut state.pending);
2559        let appended = delta::append_batch(
2560            &path,
2561            write_header,
2562            base_fingerprint,
2563            &pending,
2564            tail_fingerprint,
2565        )
2566        .map_err(StorageError::Io)?;
2567        state.log_bytes = state.log_bytes.saturating_add(appended as u64);
2568        // Clear the dirty flag *under* the delta-state lock: a racing put
2569        // pushes its frame (`record_delta`) before setting the flag, so
2570        // clearing after `drop(state)` could clobber a frame that was pushed
2571        // between the `mem::take` above and this store, leaving it stranded in
2572        // memory with nothing to force its append.
2573        self.index_checkpoint_dirty.store(false, Ordering::Relaxed);
2574        drop(state);
2575        self.persist_shard_collections_best_effort();
2576        Ok(())
2577    }
2578
2579    /// Best-effort wrapper around [`Self::persist_index_checkpoint`] — logs
2580    /// and swallows a failure rather than turning it into a hard error. The
2581    /// checkpoint is an acceleration structure; packfiles remain
2582    /// authoritative, so a failed persist only costs the next open a rescan.
2583    fn persist_index_checkpoint_best_effort(&self) {
2584        if let Err(e) = self.persist_index_checkpoint() {
2585            eprintln!("mtxdb: failed to persist index checkpoint: {e}");
2586        }
2587    }
2588
2589    /// Reads a persisted shard→collection directory directly off disk, with no
2590    /// `ShardPool`/`PackfileStorage` construction at all — the fast path
2591    /// for a `collections`-style CLI listing. Returns each collection's total record
2592    /// count summed across every shard it appears in, in insertion order.
2593    ///
2594    /// Best-effort: a missing, truncated, or corrupt file just yields an
2595    /// empty result rather than an error — the caller decides whether to
2596    /// fall back to a full scan (e.g. because this store predates the
2597    /// feature, or a writer hasn't flushed it yet).
2598    #[must_use]
2599    pub fn collection_directory_from_disk(base_dir: &std::path::Path) -> Vec<([u8; 16], u64)> {
2600        let Some(records) = read_persisted_shard_collections(base_dir).map(|d| d.records) else {
2601            return Vec::new();
2602        };
2603        let mut totals: HashMap<[u8; 16], (u64, u64)> = HashMap::new();
2604        for record in records {
2605            let entry = totals
2606                .entry(record.collection_id)
2607                .or_insert((0, record.insertion_order));
2608            entry.0 = entry.0.saturating_add(record.count);
2609            entry.1 = entry.1.min(record.insertion_order);
2610        }
2611        let mut result: Vec<([u8; 16], u64, u64)> = totals
2612            .into_iter()
2613            .map(|(collection_id, (count, order))| (collection_id, count, order))
2614            .collect();
2615        result.sort_unstable_by_key(|(collection_id, _, order)| (*order, *collection_id));
2616
2617        result
2618            .into_iter()
2619            .map(|(collection_id, count, _)| (collection_id, count))
2620            .collect()
2621    }
2622
2623    /// Reads collection summaries from the persisted shard→collection directory without
2624    /// opening packfiles. The node count and index-RAM figure match the
2625    /// index rebuilt by [`Self::open`], provided the sidecar is current.
2626    ///
2627    /// Returns `None` when the directory has not been persisted yet or is
2628    /// malformed.
2629    #[must_use]
2630    pub fn collection_summaries_from_disk(
2631        base_dir: &std::path::Path,
2632    ) -> Option<Vec<CollectionSummary>> {
2633        Self::collection_directory_persisted_at(base_dir)?;
2634        Self::collection_directory_from_disk(base_dir)
2635            .into_iter()
2636            .map(|(collection_id, nodes)| {
2637                let nodes = usize::try_from(nodes).ok()?;
2638                // A live collection always starts at `NEW_COLLECTION_INDEX_FLOOR`
2639                // (64), not `LossyIndex::new`'s own generic 16-slot floor — use
2640                // the same starting point here or a small collection's
2641                // capacity/memory estimate undershoots its real index.
2642                let capacity = u32::try_from(LossyIndex::capacity_for_entries(
2643                    nodes,
2644                    NEW_COLLECTION_INDEX_FLOOR,
2645                ))
2646                .ok()?;
2647                Some((
2648                    collection_id,
2649                    nodes,
2650                    LossyIndex::memory_usage_for_entries(nodes, NEW_COLLECTION_INDEX_FLOOR),
2651                    capacity,
2652                ))
2653            })
2654            .collect()
2655    }
2656
2657    /// Current live shard `pack_id`s per collection from the persisted shard→collection
2658    /// directory, without opening packfiles or rebuilding collection indexes.
2659    ///
2660    /// Returns `None` when the directory has not been persisted yet or is
2661    /// malformed.
2662    #[must_use]
2663    pub fn collection_shards_from_disk(
2664        base_dir: &std::path::Path,
2665    ) -> Option<HashMap<[u8; 16], Vec<u64>>> {
2666        let records = read_persisted_shard_collections(base_dir)?.records;
2667        let mut shards: HashMap<[u8; 16], Vec<u64>> = HashMap::new();
2668        for record in records {
2669            shards
2670                .entry(record.collection_id)
2671                .or_default()
2672                .push(record.pack_id);
2673        }
2674        for collection_shards in shards.values_mut() {
2675            collection_shards.sort_unstable();
2676            collection_shards.dedup();
2677        }
2678        Some(shards)
2679    }
2680
2681    /// Current live-node count per shard from the persisted shard→collection
2682    /// directory, without opening packfiles or rebuilding collection indexes.
2683    ///
2684    /// Returns `None` when the directory has not been persisted yet or is
2685    /// malformed. Callers that need an authoritative fresh count can then
2686    /// explicitly open the store and rebuild its indexes instead.
2687    #[must_use]
2688    pub fn shard_node_counts_from_disk(base_dir: &std::path::Path) -> Option<HashMap<u64, u64>> {
2689        let records = read_persisted_shard_collections(base_dir)?.records;
2690        let mut totals = HashMap::new();
2691        for record in records {
2692            let total = totals.entry(record.pack_id).or_insert(0_u64);
2693            *total = total.saturating_add(record.count);
2694        }
2695        Some(totals)
2696    }
2697
2698    /// Current number of collections with live nodes per shard from the persisted
2699    /// shard→collection directory, without opening packfiles or rebuilding collection
2700    /// indexes.
2701    ///
2702    /// Returns `None` when the directory has not been persisted yet or is
2703    /// malformed.
2704    #[must_use]
2705    pub fn shard_collection_counts_from_disk(
2706        base_dir: &std::path::Path,
2707    ) -> Option<HashMap<u64, u64>> {
2708        let records = read_persisted_shard_collections(base_dir)?.records;
2709        let mut totals = HashMap::new();
2710        for record in records {
2711            let count = totals.entry(record.pack_id).or_insert(0_u64);
2712            *count = count.saturating_add(1);
2713        }
2714        Some(totals)
2715    }
2716
2717    /// Unix-seconds timestamp of the persisted shard→collection directory, if
2718    /// one exists — lets a reader (e.g. a `collections` CLI listing) label how
2719    /// stale the counts it's showing are, the same way
2720    /// `ShardPool::stats_persisted_at` does for shard IO stats.
2721    #[must_use]
2722    pub fn collection_directory_persisted_at(base_dir: &std::path::Path) -> Option<u64> {
2723        read_persisted_shard_collections(base_dir).map(|d| d.persisted_at)
2724    }
2725
2726    /// Pin one `Arc<Shard>` per distinct shard id, keeping each shard's file
2727    /// handle (and thus its `Drop`-triggered deletion, see `Shard::drop`)
2728    /// alive for as long as the returned map is held — even if a later
2729    /// write in the same operation triggers `ShardPool::rotate` and retires
2730    /// that shard id's pool slot.
2731    ///
2732    /// This matters specifically for a scan-then-rewrite repack: without
2733    /// it, re-querying `ShardPool::get_shard` on every loop iteration can
2734    /// observe a shard that a *rotation triggered by the same repack's own
2735    /// writes* just retired-and-recreated at the same path, turning a
2736    /// stale-but-valid offset into a read into unrelated, freshly-written
2737    /// bytes.
2738    fn pin_shards(&self, shard_ids: impl Iterator<Item = u16>) -> HashMap<u16, Arc<Shard>> {
2739        let unique: HashSet<u16> = shard_ids.collect();
2740        unique
2741            .into_iter()
2742            .filter_map(|id| self.shards.get_shard(id).map(|shard| (id, shard)))
2743            .collect()
2744    }
2745
2746    /// Copy a record from an old (pinned) shard to the active shard.
2747    /// Returns the new `(hash, shard_id, offset)` or None if `old_shard_id`
2748    /// isn't in `pinned`.
2749    fn copy_record_to_shard(
2750        &self,
2751        collection_id: &[u8; 16],
2752        pinned: &HashMap<u16, Arc<Shard>>,
2753        old_shard_id: u16,
2754        old_offset: u64,
2755    ) -> Result<Option<([u8; 16], u16, u64)>, StorageError> {
2756        let Some(old_shard) = pinned.get(&old_shard_id) else {
2757            return Ok(None);
2758        };
2759        let record = self.read_at(old_shard, old_offset, true)?;
2760        let (new_shard_id, new_offset) = self.shards.put_record(&Record {
2761            collection_id: *collection_id,
2762            hash: record.hash,
2763            data: record.data,
2764        })?;
2765        Ok(Some((record.hash, new_shard_id, new_offset)))
2766    }
2767    fn swap_generation(
2768        &self,
2769        collection_id: &[u8; 16],
2770        index: LossyIndex,
2771    ) -> Result<(), StorageError> {
2772        let cache = self.generation(collection_id).map(|g| g.cache.clone());
2773        self.store_generation(collection_id, index, cache, true)
2774    }
2775
2776    /// Store a new generation for a collection, reusing the existing cache if present.
2777    ///
2778    /// If the collection is new (not yet in the `collections` map), it is added to
2779    /// `collection_order` so that [`Self::collection_summaries`] will include it, and
2780    /// any prior tombstone in `deleted.collections` is cleared so the collection
2781    /// survives a restart.
2782    ///
2783    /// `bump_generation` marks a *shape* change to the index (capacity growth,
2784    /// a from-scratch rebuild, repack, refresh) versus a plain COW update that
2785    /// preserves the previous generation. Shape changes invalidate the pending
2786    /// delta log — its frames were recorded against the old table, so they
2787    /// can't be replayed onto the checkpoint's — and the next sync rewrites the
2788    /// checkpoint instead of appending. Creating a brand-new collection also
2789    /// invalidates the log: a log that predates the collection can't express
2790    /// its records, so replaying it would silently omit every one.
2791    fn store_generation(
2792        &self,
2793        collection_id: &[u8; 16],
2794        index: LossyIndex,
2795        cache: Option<Arc<NodeCache>>,
2796        bump_generation: bool,
2797    ) -> Result<(), StorageError> {
2798        let cache = cache.unwrap_or_else(|| Arc::new(NodeCache::new(self.cache_capacity)));
2799        let is_new = self.collections.read().get(collection_id).is_none();
2800        let structural = bump_generation || is_new;
2801        if structural {
2802            self.invalidate_delta_log();
2803        }
2804        let generation = self.generation(collection_id).map_or(1, |gen| {
2805            gen.generation.saturating_add(u64::from(bump_generation))
2806        });
2807        let new_gen = Arc::new(RoomGeneration {
2808            index,
2809            cache,
2810            generation,
2811        });
2812
2813        // Clear a prior deletion marker before publishing the recreated
2814        // generation. A failure must fail the write: otherwise it appears to
2815        // succeed but disappears on the next startup scan.
2816        if is_new {
2817            if self.is_deleted_collection(collection_id) {
2818                self.clear_deleted_collection(collection_id)?;
2819            }
2820            self.collections
2821                .write()
2822                .entry(*collection_id)
2823                .or_insert_with(|| {
2824                    ArcSwap::from_pointee(RoomGeneration {
2825                        index: LossyIndex::new(0),
2826                        cache: Arc::new(NodeCache::new(self.cache_capacity)),
2827                        generation: 1,
2828                    })
2829                })
2830                .store(new_gen);
2831
2832            if !self.collection_order.read().contains(collection_id) {
2833                self.collection_order.write().push(*collection_id);
2834            }
2835        } else {
2836            // Fast path: just update the ArcSwap using a read lock on the map.
2837            // This avoids a global write lock on every single put() call.
2838            let read_guard = self.collections.read();
2839            if let Some(arc_swap) = read_guard.get(collection_id) {
2840                arc_swap.store(new_gen);
2841            } else {
2842                // Fallback in case of a race condition with a deletion
2843                drop(read_guard);
2844                self.collections
2845                    .write()
2846                    .entry(*collection_id)
2847                    .or_insert_with(|| {
2848                        ArcSwap::from_pointee(RoomGeneration {
2849                            index: LossyIndex::new(0),
2850                            cache: Arc::new(NodeCache::new(self.cache_capacity)),
2851                            generation: 1,
2852                        })
2853                    })
2854                    .store(new_gen);
2855            }
2856        }
2857        self.index_checkpoint_dirty.store(true, Ordering::Relaxed);
2858        Ok(())
2859    }
2860    /// Forces a re-scan of a collection's shards from disk and atomically swaps in the new index.
2861    /// This is designed for multi-worker environments to pull in external appends on demand.
2862    ///
2863    /// # Errors
2864    /// Returns `StorageError` if reading or parsing the underlying shards fails.
2865    pub fn refresh_collection(&self, collection_id: &[u8; 16]) -> Result<(), StorageError> {
2866        let collection_arc = self.put_mutex(collection_id);
2867        let _collection_guard = collection_arc.lock();
2868        // A refresh that publishes a *newly discovered* collection must be
2869        // excluded from a concurrent checkpoint's fingerprint→snapshot
2870        // window, exactly like a new-collection `put`/`put_many` (see the
2871        // comment on `put`). Otherwise its `rebuild_index` flush could land
2872        // the refreshed records in a pack covered by the checkpoint's
2873        // fingerprint while the collection itself is absent from the
2874        // snapshot, and a reopen trusting that checkpoint would lose them.
2875        let _create_guard = if self.generation(collection_id).is_none() {
2876            Some(self.collection_creation.read())
2877        } else {
2878            None
2879        };
2880        self.shards.discover_shards()?;
2881        let new_index = self.rebuild_index(collection_id)?;
2882        let existing_cache = self.generation(collection_id).map(|g| g.cache.clone());
2883        self.store_generation(collection_id, new_index, existing_cache, true)
2884    }
2885
2886    fn rebuild_index(&self, collection_id: &[u8; 16]) -> Result<LossyIndex, StorageError> {
2887        // Full-fallback path: a batch that exhausted growth re-scans the
2888        // packfiles. Tracked so a heavy rebuild is visible in `stats()`.
2889        self.index_rebuild_count.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
2890        // `scan_collection_records` traverses packfiles; any buffered frames
2891        // (this collection's or any other's) are invisible to it, so a fresh
2892        // flush guarantees the rebuilt index reflects every record that has
2893        // actually been put. Callers only reach this path while holding the
2894        // collection's put mutex, so nothing new can buffer for this
2895        // collection between the flush and the scan.
2896        self.shards.flush_all()?;
2897        let scanned = self.scan_collection_records(collection_id)?;
2898        let total: usize = scanned.iter().map(|(_, e)| e.len()).sum();
2899        let index = LossyIndex::new(total.saturating_mul(2).max(NEW_COLLECTION_INDEX_FLOOR));
2900        for (shard_id, entries) in scanned {
2901            for (hash, offset) in entries {
2902                // `IndexSlot` can only represent offsets up to
2903                // `PACK_INDEX_OFFSET_LIMIT`. Offsets a legacy or externally
2904                // created oversized shard can no longer fit are rejected
2905                // rather than silently skipped: writes are already capped at
2906                // `MAX_SHARD_BYTES`, so a record beyond the limit means the
2907                // shard did not come from this engine, and silently indexing
2908                // around it would make its data unreachable while reporting a
2909                // successful rebuild.
2910                check_index_offset(shard_id, &hash, offset)?;
2911                let _ = index.insert(&hash, shard_id, offset);
2912            }
2913        }
2914        Ok(index)
2915    }
2916
2917    /// Rehash a checkpoint-derived index without rescanning unrelated packs.
2918    ///
2919    /// A compact checkpoint keeps the packed `(shard, offset)` slots but not
2920    /// the 64-bit home hashes needed by [`LossyIndex::grow`].  Each occupied
2921    /// slot still names an authoritative frame whose fixed metadata prefix
2922    /// contains the full hash, so recover those identities and rehash only
2923    /// this collection's entries.  A missing shard, malformed frame, or a
2924    /// frame from another collection fails closed to the caller's existing
2925    /// whole-collection scan fallback.
2926    fn grow_checkpoint_index(
2927        &self,
2928        collection_id: &[u8; 16],
2929        index: &LossyIndex,
2930    ) -> Result<Option<LossyIndex>, StorageError> {
2931        index.grow_by_recovering_hashes(|shard_id, offset, slot_tag| {
2932            let shard = self.shards.get_shard(shard_id).ok_or_else(|| {
2933                StorageError::Corrupt(format!(
2934                    "checkpoint index refers to missing shard {shard_id}"
2935                ))
2936            })?;
2937            let (found_collection, hash) = self.shards.record_identity_at(&shard, offset)?;
2938            if &found_collection != collection_id {
2939                return Err(StorageError::Corrupt(format!(
2940                    "checkpoint index offset {offset} in shard {shard_id} belongs to another collection"
2941                )));
2942            }
2943            if LossyIndex::tag_for_hash(&hash) != slot_tag {
2944                return Err(StorageError::Corrupt(format!(
2945                    "checkpoint index tag does not match frame at offset {offset} in shard {shard_id}"
2946                )));
2947            }
2948            Ok(hash)
2949        })
2950    }
2951
2952    /// Insert with exact identity checks for checkpoint-derived slots.
2953    ///
2954    /// A compact checkpoint retains a slot's tag and location but not the
2955    /// remaining hash bits. On the rare same-tag probe, recover that frame's
2956    /// metadata, hydrate the in-memory slot, and retry. Active indexes carry
2957    /// their identities already, so their ordinary inserts take no I/O path.
2958    fn insert_index(
2959        &self,
2960        collection_id: &[u8; 16],
2961        index: &LossyIndex,
2962        hash: &NodeId,
2963        shard_id: u16,
2964        offset: u64,
2965    ) -> Result<Result<(u32, u64), InsertError>, StorageError> {
2966        loop {
2967            match index.insert_tracked(hash, shard_id, offset) {
2968                Ok(written) => return Ok(Ok(written)),
2969                Err(InsertError::TableFull) => return Ok(Err(InsertError::TableFull)),
2970                Err(InsertError::NeedsIdentity {
2971                    bucket,
2972                    shard_id,
2973                    offset,
2974                }) => {
2975                    let shard = self.shards.get_shard(shard_id).ok_or_else(|| {
2976                        StorageError::Corrupt(format!(
2977                            "index refers to missing shard {shard_id} while resolving a tag collision"
2978                        ))
2979                    })?;
2980                    let (found_collection, found_hash) =
2981                        self.shards.record_identity_at(&shard, offset)?;
2982                    if &found_collection != collection_id
2983                        || !index.hydrate_slot_identity(bucket, &found_hash)
2984                    {
2985                        return Err(StorageError::Corrupt(format!(
2986                            "index tag candidate at offset {offset} in shard {shard_id} is inconsistent"
2987                        )));
2988                    }
2989                }
2990            }
2991        }
2992    }
2993
2994    /// Fetch a node and return it as a `NodeRef` with swizzled children.
2995    ///
2996    /// # Errors
2997    /// Returns `StorageError` on I/O failure or CRC mismatch.
2998    pub fn get_swizzled(
2999        &self,
3000        collection_id: &[u8; 16],
3001        id: &NodeId,
3002        extract_children: impl Fn(&NodeData) -> Vec<NodeId>,
3003    ) -> Result<Option<NodeRef>, StorageError> {
3004        let Some(data) = self.get(collection_id, id)? else {
3005            return Ok(None);
3006        };
3007
3008        if let Some(swizzle_fn) = self.swizzle {
3009            let children = extract_children(&data);
3010            if !children.is_empty() {
3011                let cached = if let Some(gen) = self.generation(collection_id) {
3012                    gen.cache.resolve_hashes(&children)
3013                } else {
3014                    vec![None; children.len()]
3015                };
3016                let swizzled = swizzle_fn(&data, &children, &cached);
3017                if let Some(gen) = self.generation(collection_id) {
3018                    gen.cache.insert(*id, Arc::new(swizzled.clone()));
3019                }
3020                return Ok(Some(NodeRef::Resolved(*id, Arc::new(swizzled))));
3021            }
3022        }
3023
3024        Ok(Some(NodeRef::Resolved(*id, Arc::new(data))))
3025    }
3026
3027    fn resolve_from_candidates(
3028        &self,
3029        id: &NodeId,
3030        candidates: impl IntoIterator<Item = (u16, u64)>,
3031    ) -> Result<Option<NodeData>, StorageError> {
3032        let mut last_err: Option<StorageError> = None;
3033        for (shard_id, offset) in candidates {
3034            let Some(shard) = self.shards.get_shard(shard_id) else {
3035                continue;
3036            };
3037
3038            match self.read_at(
3039                &shard,
3040                offset,
3041                self.shards.checksum_policy().verifies_reads(),
3042            ) {
3043                Ok(record) => {
3044                    if record.hash != *id {
3045                        continue;
3046                    }
3047
3048                    let data = NodeData {
3049                        bytes: record.data,
3050                        children: Vec::new(),
3051                    };
3052                    // A raw mmap-backed `Bytes` is already cheap to retain
3053                    // in the caller. Do not turn every cold lookup into an
3054                    // LRU write (hash probe, lock, and eviction bookkeeping).
3055                    // The cache remains populated by writes and swizzling,
3056                    // where it stores work that cannot be recovered by a
3057                    // simple mmap range.
3058                    return Ok(Some(data));
3059                }
3060                Err(e) => {
3061                    last_err = Some(e);
3062                }
3063            }
3064        }
3065
3066        if let Some(err) = last_err {
3067            return Err(err);
3068        }
3069        Ok(None)
3070    }
3071
3072    // repack_collection_rewrite and repack_collection_topo were retired: both scanned
3073    // for records without deduplicating physical duplicates (each prior
3074    // repack's rewritten copies included), giving an exponential blowup on
3075    // repeated calls against a growing collection, and both re-fetched
3076    // Arc<Shard> per loop iteration rather than pinning shards for the
3077    // call's duration — vulnerable to the rotation race documented on
3078    // `pin_shards`. `repack_collection_reachable`'s no-live-roots fallback
3079    // subsumes both: same topological ordering, correct dedup via
3080    // `hash_to_shard_offset`, and pinned shards throughout.
3081
3082    /// BFS outward from `roots` over `extract_edges`, reading only records
3083    /// actually reached. Returns the sorted, deduplicated live hash set and
3084    /// the adjacency discovered along the way.
3085    fn bfs_live_set(
3086        pool: &ShardPool,
3087        roots: &[[u8; 16]],
3088        hash_to_shard_offset: &HashMap<[u8; 16], (u16, u64)>,
3089        pinned: &HashMap<u16, Arc<Shard>>,
3090        extract_edges: &impl Fn(&[u8; 16], &[u8]) -> Vec<[u8; 16]>,
3091    ) -> Result<AdjacencyResult, StorageError> {
3092        let mut visited: HashSet<[u8; 16]> = HashSet::new();
3093        let mut queue: VecDeque<[u8; 16]> = VecDeque::new();
3094        let mut adjacency: HashMap<[u8; 16], Vec<[u8; 16]>> = HashMap::new();
3095
3096        for root in roots {
3097            if hash_to_shard_offset.contains_key(root) && visited.insert(*root) {
3098                queue.push_back(*root);
3099            }
3100        }
3101
3102        while let Some(hash) = queue.pop_front() {
3103            let Some(&(shard_id, offset)) = hash_to_shard_offset.get(&hash) else {
3104                continue;
3105            };
3106            let Some(old_shard) = pinned.get(&shard_id) else {
3107                continue;
3108            };
3109            let record = pool.read_at(old_shard, offset, true)?;
3110            let edges = extract_edges(&hash, &record.data);
3111            for edge in &edges {
3112                if hash_to_shard_offset.contains_key(edge) && visited.insert(*edge) {
3113                    queue.push_back(*edge);
3114                }
3115            }
3116            adjacency.insert(hash, edges);
3117        }
3118
3119        let mut live_hashes: Vec<[u8; 16]> = visited.into_iter().collect();
3120        live_hashes.sort_unstable();
3121        Ok((live_hashes, adjacency))
3122    }
3123
3124    /// BFS backward (toward ancestors) from `frontier` over `extract_edges`,
3125    /// stopping expansion (and excluding from the result) anything in
3126    /// `stop_at`. Unlike [`Self::bfs_live_set`]/[`Self::bfs_bounded`]'s
3127    /// repack-side sibling, this resolves each node through `gen`'s index
3128    /// — a single frozen generation snapshot taken once before the walk
3129    /// starts — one hash lookup at a time, rather than pre-scanning every
3130    /// shard for the whole collection up front. Cost is proportional to the
3131    /// number of nodes actually walked, not collection size.
3132    ///
3133    /// Pinning `gen` for the whole walk (instead of re-resolving through
3134    /// `self.get()` per node against whatever generation happens to be
3135    /// live at call time) is also what makes this concurrency-safe: a
3136    /// repack that runs mid-walk and GCs a hash this walk already needs
3137    /// swaps in a *new* generation rather than mutating this one, so a
3138    /// node this walk has already committed to visiting can't vanish out
3139    /// from under it and get silently skipped.
3140    ///
3141    /// Returns the resolved `NodeData` for each visited node alongside the
3142    /// adjacency, so the caller has everything it needs without a second
3143    /// per-node fetch.
3144    ///
3145    /// This is an **ancestor walk with stop markers**, not a "span between
3146    /// two frontiers": a branch whose history never crosses any `stop_at`
3147    /// node is walked all the way back to the collection's true roots, not
3148    /// truncated at some implied boundary. A real from/to span would need
3149    /// `ancestors(frontier) ∩ descendants(stop_at)`, which requires
3150    /// reverse adjacency this call doesn't have — out of scope here.
3151    ///
3152    /// `limits.max_nodes`, if set, caps how many nodes this walk will visit
3153    /// before giving up on expanding further (existing queued nodes still
3154    /// finish resolving their own edges into `adjacency`, but no new nodes
3155    /// are enqueued past the cap) — the safety valve for exactly the
3156    /// runaway case above, where `stop_at` never gets hit.
3157    fn bfs_ancestors(
3158        &self,
3159        gen: &Arc<RoomGeneration>,
3160        frontier: &[[u8; 16]],
3161        stop_at: &[[u8; 16]],
3162        extract_edges: &impl Fn(&[u8; 16], &[u8]) -> Vec<[u8; 16]>,
3163        limits: WalkLimits,
3164    ) -> Result<(AdjacencyResult, HashMap<[u8; 16], NodeData>), StorageError> {
3165        let boundary: HashSet<[u8; 16]> = stop_at.iter().copied().collect();
3166        let mut visited: HashSet<[u8; 16]> = HashSet::new();
3167        let mut queue: VecDeque<[u8; 16]> = VecDeque::new();
3168        let mut adjacency: HashMap<[u8; 16], Vec<[u8; 16]>> = HashMap::new();
3169        let mut resolved: HashMap<[u8; 16], NodeData> = HashMap::new();
3170        let at_cap =
3171            |visited: &HashSet<[u8; 16]>| limits.max_nodes.is_some_and(|max| visited.len() >= max);
3172
3173        for root in frontier {
3174            if !boundary.contains(root) && !at_cap(&visited) && visited.insert(*root) {
3175                queue.push_back(*root);
3176            }
3177        }
3178
3179        while let Some(hash) = queue.pop_front() {
3180            let Some(data) = self.resolve_pinned(gen, &hash)? else {
3181                // Not actually present under this generation — nothing to
3182                // expand from and nothing to yield.
3183                continue;
3184            };
3185            let edges = extract_edges(&hash, &data.bytes);
3186            for edge in &edges {
3187                if !boundary.contains(edge) && !at_cap(&visited) && visited.insert(*edge) {
3188                    queue.push_back(*edge);
3189                }
3190            }
3191            adjacency.insert(hash, edges);
3192            resolved.insert(hash, data);
3193        }
3194
3195        // live_hashes tracks only nodes actually resolved, not everything
3196        // `visited` touched — a hash that turned out absent under `gen`
3197        // was marked visited to prevent re-queueing, but never belongs in
3198        // the CSR or the output.
3199        let mut live_hashes: Vec<[u8; 16]> = resolved.keys().copied().collect();
3200        live_hashes.sort_unstable();
3201        Ok(((live_hashes, adjacency), resolved))
3202    }
3203
3204    /// Resolves `id` within a specific, already-loaded generation snapshot
3205    /// rather than whatever generation happens to be live when called —
3206    /// the building block [`Self::bfs_ancestors`] uses to keep an entire
3207    /// walk consistent against one point-in-time view of the collection, immune
3208    /// to a repack swapping in a new generation partway through.
3209    fn resolve_pinned(
3210        &self,
3211        gen: &Arc<RoomGeneration>,
3212        id: &NodeId,
3213    ) -> Result<Option<NodeData>, StorageError> {
3214        if let Some(data) = gen.cache.get(id) {
3215            return Ok(Some((*data).clone()));
3216        }
3217        self.resolve_from_candidates(id, gen.index.lookup_all(id))
3218    }
3219
3220    /// Read every record's edges, with no reachability filtering.
3221    /// Used when a collection has no configured live roots, so nothing is known
3222    /// to be garbage. Iterates `hash_to_shard_offset` directly rather
3223    /// than the raw scan output, so it works for both full and incremental
3224    /// repack (where the map was built by merging new entries into a
3225    /// previous state rather than scanning every packfile from byte zero).
3226    fn scan_full_adjacency(
3227        pool: &ShardPool,
3228        hash_to_shard_offset: &HashMap<[u8; 16], (u16, u64)>,
3229        pinned: &HashMap<u16, Arc<Shard>>,
3230        extract_edges: &impl Fn(&[u8; 16], &[u8]) -> Vec<[u8; 16]>,
3231    ) -> Result<AdjacencyResult, StorageError> {
3232        let mut all_hashes: Vec<[u8; 16]> = hash_to_shard_offset.keys().copied().collect();
3233        all_hashes.sort_unstable();
3234        let mut adjacency: HashMap<[u8; 16], Vec<[u8; 16]>> = HashMap::new();
3235        for (hash, &(shard_id, offset)) in hash_to_shard_offset {
3236            if let Some(shard) = pinned.get(&shard_id) {
3237                let record = pool.read_at(shard, offset, true)?;
3238                let edges = extract_edges(hash, &record.data);
3239                adjacency.insert(*hash, edges);
3240            }
3241        }
3242        Ok((all_hashes, adjacency))
3243    }
3244
3245    /// Rewrite a collection's records in topological order, optionally performing
